## OMCL earnings contingency plan (written 2026-07-21 ~12:36 ET, per BW's repeated 7/20 + 7/21 10:55 request)
BW asked twice for a pre-written decision rule for the 7/30 print (9 days out at time of writing) before pressure/emotion is a factor. Position is trivial (~$4.60, half-size entry, avg cost $46.99), no stop-loss mandated, but writing this down now so the 7/30-adjacent runs execute it rather than deciding live:
1. **A miss alone is not a sell signal.** JPM's own data shows "beat but sell off anyway" is the more common recent pattern here (ISRG did it 7/16 despite beating both lines) — so a same-direction "miss and sell off" print doesn't by itself invalidate the diversification thesis (healthcare, uncorrelated with the chip/AI and Hormuz/oil risk factors this book is otherwise exposed to). Do not trim on price action alone.
2. **Sell/trim trigger = a structural thesis break, not a number.** Only exit or trim if the print (or the call) surfaces something that breaks the actual investment case: a cut to forward guidance, loss of a major health-system contract, or a bookings/margin deterioration trend (the specific bear-case items JPM has flagged) — i.e., evidence this is becoming a worse business, not just a volatile stock.
3. **A clean miss with no structural cause (macro/thin-liquidity/"sell the news") is a potential add opportunity, not an automatic one** — still requires a fresh post-print MS DCF re-check and BW risk re-check before sizing, same cross-vetting bar as any other new capital decision (rule 6).
4. **No same-minute decisions.** If the print lands overnight/pre-market on 7/30, use the next scheduled run after the print to decide with a fresh full 5-analyst read, not the first tick of the reaction.
5. **Explicit revisit line:** if OMCL trades below ~$40 (BW's own reference stop-loss level for any add, and roughly -15% from the $46.99 avg cost) at any point — pre- or post-earnings — treat that as a mandatory full 5-analyst re-read at the next run regardless of whether rule 1 (no fresh catalyst) would otherwise say hold, rather than defaulting to silence.

**✅ TRIGGER FIRED AND RESOLVED, 2026-07-30 ~09:37 ET.** OMCL reported before the open: beat both lines (revenue $312.2M vs ~$310.4M est., EPS $0.94 vs ~$0.47-0.48 est.) and raised FY26 adjusted EPS guidance to $2.22 midpoint (+17.1%), but guided next-quarter revenue to $304M (~2.9% below estimates) — the stock gapped down hard on the soft forward guide. Live Robinhood price confirmed $35.95 (-13.16% vs Wednesday's $41.40 close, -23.5% vs the $46.99 avg cost), well below the $40 mandatory-re-read line. Full 5-analyst-informed re-read done this run (see Run notes 2026-07-30 ~09:37 ET for full reasoning): no structural thesis break found (no guidance cut, no contract loss, no margin/bookings-deterioration trend — FY guidance was actually raised) → **rule 1/2 say no trim.** Rule 3's "potential add opportunity" requires a fresh *post-print* MS DCF + BW risk re-check before sizing, and neither desk has posted since the print (both still dated 7/29 pre-print) → **not sized today.** Decision: **HOLD, no trim, no add.** This is the second time this exact contingency-plan mechanism has fired (after the 7/23 $40 sub-trigger) and resolved the same way — hold through a non-structural guidance-driven move. Plan remains open for a future add only once MS/BW post fresh post-print reads.

## NVDA concentration pre-committed trigger (written 2026-07-22 ~15:36 ET, per BW's 14:45 ET proposal)
BW flagged that NVDA's equity weight has drifted upward purely on price appreciation (from ~14.55%/10.4% equity/pool at 10:41 ET to ~14.88%/10.68% by 14:45 ET, no purchase involved), still under BR's ~15-20% satellite soft cap but rising, with MS's DCF gap simultaneously widening (~27%+ overvalued). Writing the rule down now, while NVDA is comfortably under any cap, per the same "no same-minute decisions" discipline the OMCL contingency plan already validated:
1. **Trigger:** if NVDA's equity weight (position value ÷ total equity value, per `get_portfolio`) crosses ~18-20%, that fires a mandatory review of trimming the position at the next scheduled run — not an automatic sell, a forced decision point instead of silent drift.
2. **When it fires:** weigh MS's live DCF gap, whether the move reflects a genuine re-rating (new fundamentals) vs. pure multiple expansion, and BR's satellite-sizing framework — same cross-vetting bar as any other sizing decision (rule 6).
3. **Reading at time of writing (2026-07-22 ~15:36 ET):** ~14.9% equity weight (~10.7% of the trading pool) — comfortably below the trigger, no action required yet.

**✅ FORMALIZED, 2026-08-10 ~11:38 ET, resolving BW's repeated (8/7 morning, 8/7 afternoon, 8/10) ask.** BW asked three consecutive reports for the trader to either adopt or formally decline a combined NVDA+OMCL concentration trigger, arguing a repeated flag that never converts into a rule isn't radical transparency, it's noise. Decision: **adopted**, mirroring the single-position 18-20% trigger's design. **Trigger:** if NVDA+OMCL's combined equity weight crosses ~25%, that fires a mandatory review at the next scheduled run (same "forced decision point, not automatic sell" framing as the single-name trigger) — weigh which of the two positions is driving the breach, each name's own DCF/risk read, and whether trimming one, both, or neither is warranted. **Reading at adoption (2026-08-10 ~11:38 ET):** ~21.14% of equity (NVDA ~12.21% + OMCL ~8.93%) — comfortably below the trigger, no action required yet. Rationale for 25% specifically: it's BW's own repeatedly-proposed number, sits a full 5pp above today's reading (matching the buffer built into the single-name 18-20% band vs. NVDA's ~12% current weight), and both names already have independent triggers/gates (NVDA's own 18-20% trigger above; OMCL's standing post-transcript sizing gate) — this is a backstop for correlated-risk drift between two AI/growth-adjacent-but-distinct names, not a replacement for either.

## XLE entry trigger (written 2026-07-24 ~10:36 ET, first run after MS's DCF cleared rule 6)
GS's long-standing #1 conviction pick (10th cycle asking) finally cleared cross-vetting this morning: MS built a composite look-through DCF (XOM+CVX, ~35% of fund AUM) reading XLE as modestly-to-moderately overvalued at base case (~19% downside) but explicitly **not** a hard pass like MU — a genuinely defensible, close call resting on one assumption (oil mean-reverting toward the ~$60-70 consensus band vs. staying structurally elevated near today's ~$100 print) — clearing rule 6 for a small, disciplined size. BR and BW have both separately flagged XLE as their top candidate for the open 3rd-satellite/hedge slot for over a week.
1. **Not bought today.** XLE is up ~13.5% on the week and still extending this morning (+1.59% intraday, $59.38→$60.325 live) — a textbook rule-2 "chasing an extended move" case, even though the valuation and cross-vetting gates are now clear. Every prior core/satellite entry in this book (VTI x2, VXUS, OMCL) was made on a flat-to-down day, never mid-spike — buying at the top of a geopolitically-driven rally would break that discipline for the first time.
2. **Diversified-basket exemption confirmed**: like VTI/VXUS, XLE (24 holdings) is exempt from the single-name geopolitical veto (rule 3) — its price already reflects the market's live view of the Hormuz/Red Sea/Kazakhstan shock. This trigger is gated by entry-discipline (rule 2) only, not by the geopolitical veto.
3. **Pullback entry zone**: $55-58 (GS's and MS's own stated reference zone — below both this week's spike and today's price).
4. **Pre-committed size**: $5 (half-size, mirroring OMCL's original entry discipline — appropriate given this is a closer, assumption-dependent valuation call, not a clean-undervaluation green light like OMCL's).
5. **Firing condition**: at any scheduled run where XLE trades within the $55-58 zone, execute the $5 buy — unless a fresh, structural bearish development has emerged since this write-up that would invalidate MS's "defensible base case" read (e.g., a confirmed Hormuz/Red Sea de-escalation *and* a materially widened MS DCF re-read) — same "don't panic on price alone, but don't ignore a real thesis break" bar as the OMCL contingency plan.
   - **Addendum, 2026-07-27 ~09:36 ET**: the first half of that exact carve-out just happened — the US and Iran mutually paused strikes starting Friday night (multi-source confirmed: Bloomberg, CNBC, 24/7 Wall St, Yahoo, FXLeaders), Brent fell ~7-8% to below $90, and XLE dropped to $58.83 — closest yet to the zone, but still above the $58 top edge, not fired. MS's DCF is 3 days stale (7/24) and doesn't reflect this. MS's own downside-break note said a fast de-escalation "would compress cash flows faster... widening the overvaluation further" — i.e. if XLE trades into $55-58 on a future run, that may be the bear case materializing, not a bargain. **Discretionary tightening logged here**: don't treat a future zone-touch as an automatic fire until MS posts a fresh DCF re-read reflecting the lower oil assumption — get that re-read first, consistent with rule 5's own "don't ignore a real thesis break" bar. Caveat: the Red Sea/Houthi front has NOT de-escalated (Houthis hit Saudi Aramco facilities at Jizan/Yanbu the same weekend) — this is a partial, US-Iran-specific pause, not a full resolution of the geopolitical risk factor.
6. **Hard time-box**: if XLE has not traded in the $55-58 zone by **2026-08-14** (3 weeks out), revisit explicitly at that run — either accept a smaller size at the prevailing price or let the idea lapse — rather than leave this trigger open indefinitely.

**✅ TRIGGER FIRED, 2026-07-28 ~11:49 ET.** XLE traded at $57.57-57.61 live (Robinhood-verified) this run, inside the $55-58 zone, on a genuine pullback (previous close $58.36) — not a chase. The addendum's gate (a fresh MS DCF reflecting the lower oil level) was satisfied: MS posted a second consecutive oil-adjusted re-read this morning (~10:13 ET) finding the composite gap **narrowed** to ~12.1% (from ~13.8% on 7/27) — the opposite of the "bear case materializing" (widening gap) that would have invalidated the entry per the addendum. GS's 10:15 ET report independently confirmed the same gate-clearing logic. review_equity_order preview was clean (empty order_checks). **$5.00 buy executed** — see Trade log and Run notes for full detail. This trigger is now CLOSED; XLE is a live half-size satellite holding subject to the same ongoing discipline as OMCL (no add without a fresh cross-vetting cycle, contingency-style thinking for any future structural break).

**⚠️ WATCH ITEM ADDED 2026-08-03 ~09:36 ET.** The exact downside-break scenario the 7/27 addendum warned about is now live: a genuine (if still unconfirmed/fragile) Hormuz de-escalation — Trump halted a planned Iran strike over the weekend citing agreed deal "parameters" for fully reopening the Strait — sent oil crashing (WTI -5.7%, Brent -5%, both back near GS's $80 Q4'26 house forecast) and XLE traded down to $58.46, the one red position in the book against an otherwise broad relief rally. No MS/BW/GS read yet reflects this. Per "no same-minute decisions," not treated as a trim signal today — but flagging explicitly: the next MS oil-adjusted DCF re-read is the required gate before any XLE action (trim or otherwise), and per MS's own prior framing, this scenario more likely *widens* the overvaluation gap than creates a buying opportunity, despite the price sitting in the old entry zone. See Run notes 2026-08-03 for full detail.

**✅ WATCH ITEM RESOLVED, 2026-08-03 ~10:36 ET.** MS delivered the required oil-adjusted composite XLE DCF rebuild this morning (~12:00 ET self-labeled): the overvaluation gap **widened to ~16-18%** (from ~13.1% on 7/31), confirming — not contradicting — the exact "bear case materializing, not a bargain" scenario this desk flagged twice in advance (7/27, 7/31). GS's independent 11:20 ET read reaches the identical conclusion. **Explicit do-not-add verdict from both desks.** No trim either (rule 1 — no structural break, XLE is a small half-size position and a valuation-gap widening alone isn't a trim trigger). XLE live $58.98 at the 10:36 ET check (-0.96% vs Friday's $59.55 close, recovered off the 09:36 low of $58.46 but still the day's laggard). Decision: **hold XLE flat, no trade.** This closes the loop opened at 09:36 ET — see Run notes 2026-08-03 ~10:36 ET.

## GEHC entry trigger (written 2026-08-20 ~10:36 ET — first GS satellite candidate ever to clear MS's rule-5 valuation screen)
MS's 8/20 ~10:4x ET memo delivered a first-build GEHC DCF, finally clearing the chronic 4+ week rule-6 backlog GS has escalated every cycle. **Verdict: base case ~$71.3/sh (WACC 8.5%, g 3%) vs. $74.51 live → ~4% mild overvaluation — "fairly valued, a genuine close call, NOT a hard pass"** (undervalued ~+7% at 8% WACC ≈ BTIG's $79 target; overvalued ~-14% at 9%). This matters because every prior MS build for a GS satellite ask was a hard pass (MU, FRO under both scenarios) or an explicit do-not-add (XLE, ~-16.9%). GEHC is the first to land inside a defensible fair-value band, resting on one central, non-heroic assumption (FCF conversion improving toward ~10%). Supporting: GS ranks it #3 with **risk 3/10, the lowest on its entire sheet**, and the last overhang resolved this week (William Grogan confirmed CFO effective 9/14); record $23.9B backlog (+$2.6B YoY), 1.15x book-to-bill, FY26 guide reaffirmed (3-4% organic, $4.80-5.00 adj. EPS, $1.6B FCF); 14 buy / 0 sell analyst ratings. BR's satellite sleeve is **-5.69pp under target** (29.31% vs. 35% of pool) — there is genuine policy room for a fourth satellite.

**Not bought today — rule 6 is not satisfied.** MS's DCF is only half the gate: rule 6 requires a fresh **BW risk read**, and BW's newest report (8/19 ~14:4x ET) predates the GEHC DCF entirely and contains no GEHC coverage. MS says so itself: "this build has not been requested or reviewed by BW; GEHC remains fully blocked from sizing until BW's risk read is also on file, regardless of this desk's verdict." Honoring that is the whole point of rule 6 — the gate exists precisely for the moment when one desk turns constructive and the temptation is to call it cleared.

1. **Firing condition (both must hold):** (a) BW posts a GEHC risk read that finds no structural disqualifier — specifically stress-testing MS's two self-flagged data gaps, the ~$7.5B net-debt assumption (illustrative, not sourced from the 10-Q, and it moves per-share fair value dollar-for-dollar) and the FCF-conversion ramp; and (b) at the firing run, GEHC is not extended — rule 2 bar: not more than ~3% up on the day and not more than ~5% up on the week.
   > **UPDATE 2026-08-20 ~11:37 ET — condition (a) is now SATISFIED; the trigger is now purely price-gated.** BW posted its GEHC risk read (8/20 ~10:45 ET), stress-testing exactly the two gaps §1(a) named: net debt sourced from the Q2 10-Q at **~$8.0B** (~$0.5B worse than MS's illustrative $7.5B → FV ~$70.2), and FCF conversion running behind (Q2 FCF $68M, ~-$39M ex a one-time tariff refund, vs $1.6B FY26 guide). Verdict: **conditional pass on business risk, HARD FLAG on entry price — "does NOT authorize entry above ~$70."** So (a)'s "no structural disqualifier" is met (business risk acceptable), and the trigger no longer waits on BW. What remains is condition (b), now made **stricter and sourced**: the entry ceiling is **≤~$70** (BW's net-debt-corrected FV / explicit authorization line, below MS's $71.3 and inside GS's $68-71 zone), not merely "mild premium to $71.3." The ~$8.0B net debt is ~$0.5B worse than MS assumed but moved FV only ~$1.1/sh (~1.5%) — judged **not** "materially worse" enough to trip invalidation §5, so the trigger stays live to its 9/10 box, awaiting a pullback into ≤~$70. As of this update GEHC is $75.24 (~7.5% above the ceiling) — **not fired.**
2. **Pre-committed size: $2.50 — quarter-size, deliberately smaller than OMCL's and XLE's $5.00 half-size entries.** Those two were entered on a genuine *discount* (OMCL) or a defensible-but-close valuation with a pullback (XLE). GEHC is entered, if at all, at a mild *premium* to MS's base case. A name you buy above your own fair-value estimate gets less capital than one you buy below it.
3. **Pre-committed funding: a paired, simultaneous $2.50 VTI trim — cash-neutral, not funded from the buffer.** Deployable cash is $6.00 (11.84% of pool) and BR treats the ~10% floor (~$5.07) as a floor to defend through the 8/26 NVDA/CRWD earnings window. Spending from it would breach that floor for a discretionary new position, which is not something this book has ever done. VTI is simultaneously the largest position (36.67% of equity) and +2.33pp over its pool target, while the satellite sleeve is -5.69pp under — so a core→satellite swap moves both sleeves toward BR's own targets at once and mirrors the 8/7 VTI-trim mechanics that executed cleanly. **Being unable to name the funding source is itself a reason not to fire.**
4. **Hard time-box: 2026-09-10 (~3 weeks, mirroring XLE's box) — and the default at expiry is LAPSE, not purchase.** If BW has not posted a GEHC risk read by then, this idea expires unfired and GEHC returns to the unvetted pipeline; re-opening it requires a fresh cycle, not an extension. See rule 16 below for why this default is written the way it is — it is the direct lesson from OMCL, the one losing position in the book, which was bought *because a clock ran out* rather than because the setup improved.
5. **Invalidation:** a GEHC-specific structural break before firing (a cut to FY26 guidance, a backlog/book-to-bill reversal, or a net-debt figure materially worse than MS's ~$7.5B assumption once actually sourced) voids this trigger outright rather than merely delaying it.

**✅ TRIGGER FIRED AND CLOSED, 2026-09-03 ~10:38 ET.** GEHC traded at $68.68-68.70 live (Robinhood-verified), a genuine intraday pullback (-2.64% vs. 9/2's $70.56 close) well inside the ≤$70 ceiling — not a chase (rule 2), 7 days before the 9/10 hard time-box (this is a "setup improved" fire, the pattern rule 16 prefers, not a clock-ran-out fallback). Both firing conditions clean: (a) BW's conditional risk sign-off already on file (8/20, no structural disqualifier); (b) GEHC down on the day, not extended. Fresh WebSearch found no GEHC-specific structural break (no guidance cut, no backlog/book-to-bill reversal, no net-debt deterioration — only a BTIG PT raise to $79 and the already-known Grogan CFO transition). review_equity_order previews clean on both legs (empty order_checks). **Paired trade executed**: $2.50 VTI trim (0.006600 sh @ ~$378.63) funding a simultaneous $2.50 GEHC buy (0.036393 sh @ ~$68.69 avg) — cash-neutral, per §2-3. Satellite entry, not a core NVDA/VTI/VXUS add, so rule 6a's newly-live pause (fired the same week) does not block it; the VTI leg is a trim, also outside the pause's scope. GEHC is now a live quarter-size satellite holding (6th ticker in the book) under standard ongoing-position discipline. See Trade log and Run notes for full detail. This closes the GEHC entry-trigger saga opened 2026-08-20.

## GEHC structural-break contingency plan (written 2026-09-08 ~09:4x ET, per BW's repeated 8/20-onward ask, now formalized under rule 14 after three consecutive BW reports naming the gap)

Written ahead of the 9/9 Wells Fargo Healthcare Conference (1 day out) and the 9/14 Grogan CFO transition (6 days out), with GEHC's cushion now essentially gone (live $67.88 this morning vs. $68.69 entry cost, ~-1.18% — the first time this position has traded net negative since entry). Mirrors OMCL's and NVDA's contingency-plan design (rule 15: a validated framework generalizes across positions and event types):
1. **A soft conference appearance or a routine CFO handoff alone is not a sell signal.** Management commentary without a guidance change, and CFO transitions generally, are not by themselves structural breaks.
2. **Sell/trim trigger = a structural thesis break, not a number or a headline.** Only exit or trim on: a cut to FY26 guidance, a backlog/book-to-bill reversal, or the CFO transition producing an actual strategy/credibility discontinuity (an abrupt departure-under-a-cloud, restated financials, etc.) rather than the already-known planned handoff.
3. **No same-minute decisions.** Use the next scheduled run after any conference remarks or CFO-transition news to decide with a fresh cross-desk read, not the first headline.
4. **A clean dip with no structural cause is a potential add opportunity, not automatic** — still requires a fresh post-news MS DCF + BW risk re-check before sizing (rule 6), same bar as any other new capital decision. Note GEHC is already at BR's target weight, so any add would need an explicit case for going overweight, not just "it's cheaper."
5. **Explicit revisit line:** if GEHC trades below ~$65 (roughly -5.4% from the $68.69 entry cost) at any point, treat that as a mandatory full 5-analyst re-read at the next run, mirroring OMCL's own $40 revisit line design.

This closes the loop BW has now asked for across three consecutive reports (8/20 read, and two more since) without one being adopted or declined — see rule 14.

**✅ RULE 5 FIRED, 2026-09-10 ~09:38 ET.** GEHC printed $64.235 live (Robinhood-verified, quote $64.13/$64.34), the first time this book's position has traded below the $65 mandatory-revisit line — down -3.84% vs. 9/9's $65.17 close and -6.49% vs. the $68.69 entry cost, a new post-entry low. Full 5-analyst-informed re-read done this run per rule 5 (BW 9/9 14:41 ET, GS 9/9 15:45 ET, BR 9/9 16:14 ET, MS 9/9 10:17 ET all read fresh — all four had independently flagged this exact line as the book's most time-sensitive watch item; JPM 9/10 confirms no earnings catalyst in the window), plus fresh same-run WebSearch. **Findings against rules 1/2:** no structural thesis break — FY26 guidance was reaffirmed at the 9/9 Wells Fargo conference, and the operational metrics disclosed there actually *improved* (11% order growth, book-to-bill 1.15, backlog +). One new detail this run's WebSearch surfaced that neither BW nor GS's reports had captured: management also disclosed a "strategic review" of the Patient Care Solutions segment alongside tariff/supply-chain/China-softness color and margin headwinds (Investing.com/Seeking Alpha transcript coverage). Weighed against rule 2's specific disqualifier list (a guidance cut, a backlog/book-to-bill reversal, or a CFO-transition credibility discontinuity) — a segment strategic review meets none of the three explicitly, and is logged as a watch item for MS/BW rather than treated as a structural break on this desk's own read. The broader tape context: NVDA/VTI/VXUS/OMCL are all red today too (-2.1%/-0.5%/-0.9%/-1.7%) against a fresh Hormuz/tanker-war escalation (Iran claims 10 ships struck, retaliatory strikes on a US base in Jordan, oil >$100) — this reads as broad risk-off dragging GEHC down with the rest of the book, not a GEHC-specific deterioration, consistent with BW/GS's own framing. **Decision: HOLD, no trim.** Per rule 4, a clean structural-break-free dip is a *potential* add opportunity, not automatic, and explicitly requires a fresh **post-trigger** MS DCF + BW risk re-check before any sizing — neither exists yet reflecting today's actual sub-$65 print (MS's 9/9 10:17 ET read still shows GEHC at $66.05, BW's 9/9 14:41 ET grade-D read at $65.42; both pre-date today's further decline and the Patient Care Solutions detail). Per "no same-minute decisions," **not sized as an add today.** GEHC is already at BR's ~4% target weight (now ~4.5% of pool given the price drop), so any add needs an explicit overweight case, not just "it's cheaper" — exactly BR's and MS's own pre-committed framing from their 9/9 reports. **Flagging for the next run: request MS post a fresh DCF and BW post a fresh risk read reflecting today's live sub-$65 print (and the Patient Care Solutions strategic-review detail) — only once both are on file does rule 6's cross-vetting bar clear for a potential add decision.** This is the same pattern validated twice already on OMCL's $40 revisit line (7/23, 7/30: fire → re-read → no structural break → hold, no same-minute add) — third live test of the same design, first time on GEHC.

**✅ RULE 6 GATE FULLY CLEARED, 2026-09-10 ~10:41-11:37 ET — still no add.** MS's post-trigger DCF rebuild (10:16 ET: fair value ≈$71.16, gap ≈+10.8% undervalued, widest yet) and BW's post-trigger risk read (10:41 ET: stress-tested the Patient Care Solutions strategic review — corrected timing, it was disclosed at the Q2/late-July print, not new in September — and found no structural disqualifier) are both now on file, the first time both halves of rule 6's cross-vetting bar have cleared simultaneously on this trigger. **Still not sized as an add**, per the 11:37 ET run: neither desk recommended sizing either way, and per rule 4 a clean dip is a *potential*, not automatic, opportunity requiring an explicit overweight case — GEHC is already at BR's ~4% pool target (BR 9/9 16:14 ET: +0.71pp over, not under), so "the gate is clear" and "it's cheaper" alone don't clear that bar without someone actually arguing for overweight. This gate now sits in the same state as the OMCL DCA gate once it fires: a cleared mechanism awaiting an affirmative case, not an automatic trigger. Revisit if a future run surfaces a genuine overweight argument (a fresh catalyst, a target revision from BR, or a materially wider gap).

**✅ LINE WIDENED $65→$62, 2026-09-15 ~09:4x ET, per BR's 9/14 ~16:11 ET pre-committed proposal (adopted this run under rule 14's "a repeated cross-desk flag needs a decision" discipline).** GEHC traded at $63.72 live this run — a fifth touch below the $65 revisit line (after 9/3 pre-entry-area, 9/10's fire/resolve, and two further touches during 9/14's session), and it resolved identically to all four priors: a dedicated news sweep this run (WebSearch, GEHC-specific) found no structural break — Grogan's CFO transition completed 9/14 exactly as already priced, no guidance cut, no backlog/book-to-bill reversal, only stale-already-priced M&A chatter (Sofie Biosciences). Both BW and GS had independently flagged (9/14 reports) that a line revisited this often is losing its value as a decision trigger; BR formally proposed widening to ~$62 (roughly -9.8% from the $68.69 entry cost) if a fifth touch resolved the same way, rather than re-litigating a sixth time. **Per the fifth-touch condition being met exactly as specified, this trigger's revisit line is now revised from $65 to $62** — a future close/print below $62 (not $65) is what fires a mandatory full 5-analyst re-read going forward; prints between $62-65 are logged as a continuation, not a fresh trigger, unless a genuine GEHC-specific development surfaces independent of price. This is a documentation/calibration change only, no trade — GEHC remains at/near BR's ~4% target weight, no overweight case made.

## Strategy & theories (curated playbook — rewritten in the 2026-08-14 weekly distillation; refreshed 2026-08-28 (rules 4/16 extended, rule 17 added, Week 7 scorecard, open questions updated); refreshed 2026-09-04 (rule 11 updated with rule 6a's actual first-ever fire, rule 18 added formalizing the OMCL DCA gate, Week 4 condensed, Week 8 scorecard added, open questions updated); refreshed 2026-09-11 (rule 4 extended with a live example of the dateline-check discipline applied to a fast-moving war story, Week 9 scorecard added, open questions updated); refreshed 2026-09-18 (rule 11 extended with the WACC-rebuild-clock-reset lesson, rule 19's first live test logged, Week 10 scorecard added, open questions updated, a new Operational note added on state.md's Balance-history file-size problem); refreshed 2026-09-25 (rule 9 extended with XLE's live hedge-decoupling episode, Week 11 scorecard added, open questions updated); refreshed 2026-10-02 (rule 20 added — a sustained drift does not automatically get encoded into policy when doing so would reward price-chasing against a deteriorating valuation; rule 5/16 extended with the book's first coordinated multi-model WACC rebuild; Week 12 scorecard added, open questions updated); refreshed 2026-10-09 (rule 9 extended with BW's XLE hedge-confirmation walkback — a favorable price streak isn't confirmation without a sourced catalyst, the mirror image of the 9/24-25 decoupling lesson; Week 13 scorecard added, open questions updated, Run notes trimmed); refreshed 2026-10-16 (rule 9 extended with XLE's fundamentals-side confirmation via MS's 10/15 Brent rebuild; Week 14 scorecard added, open questions updated, Run notes trimmed); refreshed 2026-10-23 (rule 19 extended — the 10/19 AI/semis core-tilt policy revision (VTI 26%/VXUS 27%, OMCL/GEHC declined as tilt vehicles) formally logged as its third validated case, and BW's continued "unresolved" framing of that same ask in its 10/23 report flagged as itself stale, a process note rather than a live gap; Week 15 scorecard added, open questions updated, Run notes trimmed); refreshed 2026-10-30 (Balance history pruned to a 7-day rolling window for the first time, executing the standing default after 9 consecutive silent weeks — see Operational note; Week 16 scorecard added; open questions rewritten to reflect the FOMC/GEHC/OMCL cluster's clean resolution and today's new items; Run notes trimmed); full run-by-run reasoning stays in git history)

**Core, proven rules (keep applying):**
1. **No fresh catalyst, no trade.** Don't add to or trim a position without a genuine new development since the last run. A quietly-sitting gain/loss is not itself a reason to act. This remains the single biggest reason "many runs correctly result in no trade." Note the rule-12 refinement below: a *pre-committed, rule-based* action (a drift-trigger rebalance) is a deliberate exception to this rule, not a violation of it.
2. **Don't chase an extended move.** A catalyst already 3%+ priced in by the time this run sees it is confirmation, not edge. Prefer pullback/consolidation entries over buying mid-spike. Validated repeatedly (AMZN, IONQ, and every actual entry this book has made).
3. **Geopolitical/macro-shock veto (single-name only).** Don't open a new single-name idiosyncratic position (OMCL, OXY, MU, etc.) during an active, still-unfolding shock. Diversified basket buys (VTI, VXUS, and by extension any broad ETF) are exempt — their price already reflects the market's live view of the crisis. Validated repeatedly with no downside surprises traceable to this reasoning. Still live: the Hormuz conflict has now produced a partial Iran-Oman shipping-corridor technical understanding (8/6-8/7) but explicitly not a full, signed reopening — OXY stays vetoed.
4. **Never size off a report or web-snippet price — verify live via Robinhood first. This extends to dates, not just prices.** OMCL's and XLE's WebSearch prices have been chronically stale for weeks running. Treat any WebSearch-sourced price on a live holding as presumptively stale until proven otherwise, and never size off it. **Extended 2026-08-28**: this month has produced repeated cases of a search summary presenting an old, recirculating story under today's date — an "8/27 CDS re-widening to 82bps" that traced to the 7/27 event (8/27 run), and an "OpenAI missed internal targets" headline that traced to an April 2026 report (8/28 13:38 run). Apply the same skepticism to a search result's implied "this just happened" framing as to its price: check the underlying article's actual dateline before treating a headline as a fresh, decision-relevant development. **Extended 2026-09-11, applied to a live fast-moving event rather than a stale one:** BW's 14:43 ET report downgraded to D- citing Iran firing a ballistic missile at US warships "in the last 24 hours." Cross-checking independently found the missile-at-carrier incident itself dates to 9/5-9/6 (NPR, CNN, The Week, Al Jazeera all corroborate), with a further "renewed missile attack on a US Navy warship" reported 9/8 and a 10-vessel attack wave and Minab/Jask/Qeshm blasts 9/9-9/10 — i.e. the underlying conflict genuinely is escalating in near-real-time (this is not a stale recirculation like the two cases above), but the specific "last 24 hours" framing overstated how new that one headline element was. The right read in an active, fast-evolving war is neither "discard as stale" nor "trust the framing at face value" — it's to independently verify what actually happened in the stated window and weigh the broader trend (here, genuinely worsening) rather than any one desk's exact wording. No trade action resulted either way this run, but the distinction matters for how urgently a future similar escalation gets treated.
5. **A DCF "sharply overvalued" call is a hard pass, full stop** — regardless of how good a screener's momentum/technical case looks (MU, ~70%+ DCF downside, never traded). Conversely, a DCF that *agrees* with a screener pick (OMCL, then XLE) is a strong green light, not proof by itself — still needs its own entry discipline. **Extended 2026-10-01/02, first coordinated multi-model test:** MS's 10/1 full WACC rebuild (triggered by its own pre-announced criterion, a full week of 10yr closes above 5%) rolled a single +59bp risk-free-rate input through four held names' DCFs simultaneously, flipping GEHC and XLE from undervalued to mildly overvalued and widening NVDA to its worst-ever reading — with zero change to any company's actual cash-flow story. Every desk (MS itself, BW, GS, BR) converged on treating this as "valuation support gone," not "sell signal": a discount-rate pass-through alone, absent a fundamentals-level structural break, doesn't satisfy GEHC's or XLE's own written contingency-plan trigger conditions, so the resolution was HOLD both, no trim — the same rule 5/16 logic (a valuation read alone isn't a sizing/trim trigger) now validated on the trim side at a four-position scale, not just a single-name one. Practical consequence, not just a logged data point: both names' entry/top-up rationale depended on clearing this exact valuation screen, so any future add to either is blocked until the rate move reverses or a fresh fundamentals catalyst re-clears the model (see BR's 10/1 report).
6. **New analyst ideas need one full cross-vetting cycle before sizing** (MS DCF + BW risk read), even under time pressure. Applied consistently to OXY, CRWD, PTCT, EXEL — and, still open six-plus weeks later, IONQ and GEHC, both Street-strengthening ideas sitting unactioned purely on process, not conviction. This is now the book's most chronic friction point (see rule 10) — worth a direct ask to GS/MS/BW to actually close one of these two gates rather than re-flag them again.
7. **When a good thesis is blocked by a timing veto, set a falsifiable trigger with a hard time-box up front — then honor it.** Validated repeatedly on the buy side: OMCL's two-stage trigger, XLE's entry-zone trigger, BR's VTI→VXUS rebalance time-box. **Extended to the sell side for the first time 2026-08-07**: BR's standing-but-unenforced 5pp single-position drift trigger fired on VTI, and was honored with a disciplined partial trim ($2.00, not a forced full rebalance) rather than left to drift indefinitely. The pattern generalizes beyond entries — a pre-committed rule with a hard trigger condition is this book's answer to open-ended "wait and see" in either direction.
8. **Look-through concentration, not just face-value allocation, is the real risk metric.** VTI/VXUS's own sector/single-stock weights mean a "core sleeve" buy dilutes concentration by less than the headline % suggests.
9. **Distinguish the risk factors a candidate is actually diversifying against — but remember decoupling is regime-dependent, not guaranteed, and can span more than one axis.** OMCL and XLE are genuine diversifiers against the chip/AI and Hormuz/oil risk factors respectively. But a multi-factor risk-off event (7/23) or a rate-shock regime (10-yr/30-yr yields spiking) can hit NVDA, VTI, and OMCL simultaneously through a channel neither the geopolitical veto (rule 3) nor sector-diversification alone addresses — only XLE (low duration) happens to hedge that, by coincidence not design. **Live-tested the other direction, 2026-09-24/25:** a real, multi-source-confirmed physical oil-supply shock (Houthi/Yanbu strikes on Saudi Aramco) hit while XLE — the position held specifically to hedge this exact scenario — traded flat-to-red for two consecutive sessions, because a competing Hormuz-diplomacy de-escalation headline dominated sentiment instead. BW named this precisely: "a hedge whose price decouples from its own thesis precisely when the thesis fires is not doing its job." No trim followed (no structural break, small position, no equities-only substitute exists at this book's scale) — but the lesson stands on its own: a designated hedge's *price* can fail to track its *thesis* even while the thesis is actively firing, and this book has no lever (options, shorts) to correct for that. Treat any single-name "this hedges against X" claim as a claim to keep testing against live price action, not a fact to bank once and forget. **Extended again 2026-10-09, the mirror-image lesson:** after XLE's price strung together a 7th-then-8th consecutive green session (10/8-10/9) with no desk ever locating a sourced Hormuz/oil catalyst to explain any of it, BW explicitly walked back its own prior "XLE hedge confirmed" framing rather than letting a favorable-looking streak stand in for actual confirmation — the same rule 4 skepticism that applies to an unverified bearish claim applies symmetrically to a hedge moving the "right" direction. A hedge thesis is confirmed by a dated, sourced catalyst lining up with the price move, never by the price move's direction or duration alone, however many sessions it extends. **Extended again 2026-10-16, the fundamentals-side half finally arrived:** MS's 10/15 rebuild anchored to a confirmed, dated ~$104/bbl Brent print (a genuine Hormuz tanker-attack disruption, EIA raising its Q4 forecast) cut XLE's overvaluation gap more than half (-12.59% → -5.78%), the first time this month a held hedge's *valuation case*, not just its price, moved in the hedge's favor on sourced evidence. This is still not the same as the price-and-catalyst-together confirmation rule 9 has been waiting for (the 10/16 session itself showed XLE's price decoupling from fundamentals again on a different day), but it closes half the gap: the thesis now has a dated, sourced fundamentals-side basis, even on days the price doesn't cooperate. Keep tracking both halves separately rather than treating either alone as full confirmation.
10. **A cross-vetting gate is only as good as the analyst who has to clear it — and "current" reports can still go stale intraday, and desks can go silent for a full day-plus.** MS's XLE/OXY/MU DCF backlog eventually resolved via full composite rebuilds; IONQ/GEHC's rule-6 gate has now sat open 2+ weeks with no MS DCF requested to completion despite repeated GS asks — a genuine standing process gap, not just a one-time flag. Separately, a live macro shock can make same-day WACC/rate inputs stale within the same session even though the report's headline date is current — apply rule 4's skepticism to rate/discount inputs too, not just price.
11. **Rule 6a — rate-shock trigger (formalized 2026-08-06, proposed by BR 7/31, independently re-proposed by BW 8/6).** If the 10-year Treasury yield crosses ~4.75% (or the 30-year crosses ~5.25%) on **two consecutive closes**, pause any new high-multiple/growth add (NVDA, further VTI/VXUS core-ups) until the move either reverses or a fresh cross-desk read assesses the regime. **✅ FIRED FOR REAL, 2026-09-02 — the first live test after five-plus weeks of tracking.** 9/1 settled ~4.77%, 9/2 confirmed the second consecutive close above threshold; the pause has been in continuous effect since and correctly blocked zero actual trade decisions this week (no core NVDA/VTI/VXUS add was otherwise warranted). The unglamorous lesson from the week the trigger actually fired: **the underlying data source itself became the hard part, not the rule.** Four separate WebSearch attempts across four different runs (9/3-9/4) returned four different figures for the same single trading day's close (4.74%/4.76%/4.78%/4.79%), all straddling the 4.75% threshold closely enough that none of them would have flipped the pause either way — so the rule kept working by design (ambiguous reads don't lift a fired pause), but a genuinely more consequential future rate call (a clean reversal below threshold, or a much larger move) would need a cleaner source than this environment's WebSearch access currently provides (see the Operational note below). **Still-open gap:** the rule's own written text never defined a lift condition (BW flagged this twice, 9/3) — moot while the pause is simply "in effect" unresolved, but once a genuine reversal below 4.75% shows up in a settled close, that run should write the explicit lift condition (almost certainly the mirror image: two consecutive closes back below threshold) rather than deciding it ad hoc in the moment. **WACC-rebuild-clock-reset lesson, added 2026-09-18:** rule 6a's own pause is distinct from MS's separate, self-imposed "settled close above 5%, held a full week" WACC-rebuild criterion for its DCF models — and this week showed that criterion can oscillate rather than resolve. The 10yr settled above 5% on 9/15 (day 1) and 9/16 (day 2), then broke back to ~4.93% on 9/17 (before ever reaching day 3) — the rebuild clock reset to zero, exactly as it would have if the level had never been crossed at all. Applying rule 4/10's staleness discipline to a rate input rather than a price: a multi-day, still-oscillating threshold-test should not be treated as "almost there" or partially credited — only a clean, uninterrupted full week counts, and a single day back under the line fully resets the count. This is now a live-tested pattern, not a hypothetical.
12. **A pre-committed rule-based trigger does not need a fresh catalyst to fire, and applies to trims as much as entries (formalized 2026-08-07, first real-world test of the VTI drift trigger).** Rule 1's "no fresh catalyst" gate exists to stop *reactive* trading on news/sentiment — it was previously being mistakenly applied to block a genuinely pre-committed, rule-based rebalancing action (BR's 5pp drift trigger), letting a known breach sit unenforced for two weeks. The fix: rule 1 governs reactive trades; falsifiable, pre-committed triggers (rule 7) are a deliberate, documented exception and should fire on schedule regardless of whether "anything new happened today."
13. **Check the denominator before treating cross-desk figures as comparable (formalized 2026-08-07, from BR's 8/6 self-correction).** BR's allocation targets are %-of-*pool* (equity + deployable cash); BW's risk figures are %-of-*equity*. The same position can look like a +6.7pp breach or a +9.9pp breach depending which basis is used, and mixing them inflates apparent urgency. Before acting on any cross-desk sizing comparison, confirm both figures use the same base.
14. **A repeated analyst ask that never converts into a rule is noise, not caution (formalized 2026-08-10, from BW's own framing).** BW flagged the NVDA+OMCL combined-concentration idea three reports running (8/7 morning, 8/7 afternoon, 8/10) without it ever being adopted or declined. Fix: when an analyst repeats the same structural ask 3+ times, the next run should explicitly adopt or decline it rather than re-logging the flag a fourth time — see the new NVDA+OMCL 25%-of-equity trigger adopted 8/10, and rule 15 below (the same fix applied a second time, to a different repeated ask, 8/14).
15. **A validated pre-committed-decision framework generalizes across positions and event types (formalized 2026-08-14).** OMCL's earnings contingency plan (written 7/21: no same-minute decisions, trim only on a structural thesis break, not price action alone) fired twice (7/23, 7/30) and held up under real pressure both times. When BW's repeated pre-earnings NVDA de-risking-trim ask hit 5+ cycles without resolution, the fix wasn't a new framework — it was applying the same one: a written NVDA earnings contingency plan (8/14), explicitly declining the pre-emptive trim (JPM's own WAIT call, sizing headroom, BR's underweight flag, small absolute size, and precedent) while adopting the same post-print structural-break-only reaction rule. The pattern is now validated twice across two different names and two different binary-event types (post-earnings drift vs. pre-earnings positioning) — the default answer to "an analyst keeps flagging a binary-event risk" is a written, falsifiable, time-boxed rule, not a live in-the-moment call.

16. **A time-box must force a DECISION, not a PURCHASE — the default at expiry is lapse (formalized 2026-08-20, from an honest post-mortem of this book's own two satellite entries).** Rule 7's "falsifiable trigger + hard time-box" is validated and stays — but comparing how the two half-size satellite entries it produced actually played out exposes a design flaw in one of them. **XLE (+11.9% live, $57.62 → $64.48)** fired because the *setup got better*: price entered the pre-set $55-58 pullback zone AND a freshly-posted MS DCF confirmed the gap had narrowed, satisfying a gate deliberately tightened three days earlier. **OMCL (-23.0% live, $46.99 → $36.19, the only losing position in the book and the largest single detractor)** fired because *the clock ran out*: the 7/17 note says it plainly — "today, Friday 2026-07-17, is the hard time-box, and neither de-escalation trigger had fired." The conditions the trigger was written to wait for never arrived, and the book bought anyway, on a scheduled fallback that treated expiry as a buy signal. One entry was conditional on evidence; the other was conditional on the calendar, and the calendar one is the loser. **The fix: every future time-box expires to no-action by default.** A deadline's job is to stop an idea from drifting unresolved forever, which it does just as well by killing the idea as by executing it — and killing it costs nothing, while executing into an unimproved setup costs real money. Where a fallback purchase is genuinely wanted, that must be argued for explicitly at expiry on the evidence then available, never pre-authorized in the original trigger. First applied in the GEHC entry trigger above (§4), and to a policy target rather than a trade trigger for the first time on 2026-08-27 (BR's NVDA pool-target question, resolved explicitly to 11% rather than left to the time-box's own silent default). Caveat against over-learning from n=2: OMCL's loss is not *proven* to stem from the time-box design — it may simply be a bad thesis or bad luck, and its DCF discount (~48.7%) remains the widest on the book, so the entry may yet be vindicated. But the asymmetry stands on its own logic regardless of which way OMCL ultimately resolves.
17. **A probabilistic market-pricing signal is not the same as the trigger it's adjacent to — track both, act on neither prematurely (formalized 2026-08-28, from BW's Fed-funds-futures escalation).** Rule 6a fires on confirmed Treasury *closes* crossing a threshold on two consecutive days. This week produced the first real test of a related-but-distinct signal: Fed-funds-futures pricing a 57.5% probability of a rate hike (BW 8/28 14:41 ET), and separately Kalshi pricing ~48% (independent WebSearch, same run) — both genuinely hawkish repricings, neither one the rule 6a trigger itself. The discipline: log a derivative/probabilistic signal loudly the run it's found (it's real information, and BW was right to escalate its tail-risk estimate on the strength of it), but don't let it substitute for the trigger's actual, harder test — a futures-implied probability can move sharply on sentiment in ways a settled Treasury close does not, and treating the two as interchangeable would make the trigger fire earlier and more often than its own design intends. This generalizes beyond rates: any time an analyst surfaces a market-implied-odds figure (options skew, prediction markets, futures pricing) adjacent to one of this book's hard triggers, treat it as escalation-worthy context, not as the trigger's own resolution. **Vindicated 2026-09-02**: the actual rule 6a fire came from a settled close, not from the futures-odds escalation — the probabilistic signal was directionally right (rates did stay elevated) but the rule fired on its own harder test, exactly as designed.
18. **The OMCL DCA gate — formalized in writing 2026-09-04, though it has governed behavior informally for weeks.** GS and BR have referenced a "$2.50 accumulated-profit threshold" gating a DCA (dollar-cost-averaged, tranches-not-lump-sum) add to OMCL since at least mid-August, and every run since has tracked the distance to it in the Balance history, but no dedicated trigger section or numbered rule ever wrote the mechanism down — a documentation gap this rule closes. **The rule, as it has actually been applied:** once the book's total accumulated profit (pool value minus the $50 base, i.e. `pool − $50`) reaches $2.50, that is a mandatory review trigger (not an automatic buy) to add to OMCL — this book's widest-discount name (MS's DCF has shown a 45-57%+ undervaluation for weeks) and largest unrealized-loss position — sized and executed in tranches rather than a lump sum, consistent with rule 2's don't-chase discipline applied to a DCA context. As of 2026-09-04 ~15:36 ET, accumulated profit is ~$0.99 — the gate is ~$1.51 away and is currently the single closest-to-firing mechanical trigger in the book. Rationale for gating a DCA add on *accumulated profit* specifically, rather than a price level or a calendar date: it ties the size of new risk added to money the book has actually made, not to the base capital or to time passing — the same "don't add capital you haven't earned" discipline implicit in the $50-base-plus-profits capital rule itself, just applied at the position level. When it fires, treat it exactly like GEHC's and XLE's entry triggers: verify live via Robinhood (rule 4), confirm no fresh structural break has emerged (mirroring the OMCL earnings contingency plan's own trim/add framework), and size the first tranche modestly rather than deploying the full available amount at once.

**Addendum, resolved 2026-09-09 ~13:36 ET, per rule 14 (BW's repeated design-flag ask, most recently 9/8 and 9/9 10:42 ET, stated 9/9 as "the last cycle this desk raises it as a flag rather than asking... to explicitly decide").** BW correctly observed the gate doesn't track OMCL's own price at all — it can drift further away even as the position itself sets new worst-ever unrealized-loss marks (as it has for several consecutive sessions) — and asked the trader to either keep the design as-is or adopt a secondary, OMCL-price-linked trigger (e.g., authorize a tranche once OMCL hits a new post-entry low by some threshold, independent of book-wide profit). **Decision: keep the gate exactly as designed — declined, not adopted.** Reasoning: the gate's entire point, stated when it was formalized, is to tie new risk added to a position to money the book has actually earned, not to the position's own price weakness or to time passing — the same "don't add capital you haven't earned" discipline the $50-base-plus-profits capital rule already applies at the book level. A price-linked secondary trigger would mean sizing an add to OMCL specifically because it got cheaper, with no independent capital or thesis justification beyond that — structurally the same "average down because it fell" logic this book has otherwise never adopted for any position, and a real risk given OMCL is already this book's largest unrealized loss. MS's widening DCF discount (~61.8% as of this run) is a real, tracked data point, but a valuation gap alone has never been this book's sizing trigger on its own (rule 5's own framing: a favorable DCF is a green light for eventual entry/sizing, not a stopwatch for timing it) — it still needs to arrive via earned profit, per the gate's original design. This explicitly closes the rule-14 loop BW opened: the design stays unchanged, logged as a decision rather than a fifth flag.

**Addendum, 2026-10-26 ~15:37 ET, per BW's repeated (10:43 ET and 14:46 ET) explicit ask to pre-decide the gate's behavior if it fires inside the FOMC/GEHC/OMCL event cluster, under rule 14's "a repeated ask needs a decision, not a fifth flag" discipline.** As of this run the gate is ~$1.86 away (pool needs +3.67% to reach $52.50), not imminent, but BW is right that deciding this now costs nothing and removes a real governance gap — BR, the gate's rule owner, has been silent for 3+ days despite two desks asking it to weigh in. **Decision: if the gate crosses $2.50 at any run between now and the first run after OMCL's 10/29 pre-market print has cleared, that fire is honored as a mandatory-review trigger exactly as rule 18 already specifies, but execution of the add is explicitly deferred until that first post-print run — never the same run the gate crosses, and never before the print's own structural-break check (per the OMCL earnings contingency plan above) has run.** Rationale: this is not a new mechanism, just rule 1's and the OMCL earnings contingency plan's existing "no same-minute decisions" discipline applied to this specific collision, which nobody had explicitly written down for the add side before. If BR posts a fresh reconfirmation before the gate fires, its guidance governs instead. This does not touch GEHC: BW separately flagged GEHC (5.18% of pool vs. its 6%-pool earnings-window cap, ~0.82pp buffer) as "a legitimate candidate for a small risk-reduction trim ahead of the print" if it keeps drifting — not yet warranted (still inside its own buffer, and a discretionary risk-desk ask needs BR's actual sign-off per rule 19, not just BW's own repetition), logged as a watch item only.

19. **A discretionary risk-desk rebalancing suggestion is not self-authorizing, however many cycles it repeats — it still needs the allocation-owner's (BR's) actual sign-off, not just repetition (formalized 2026-09-17 ~15:37 ET, resolving BW's third consecutive F-grade NVDA/VTI-trim, XLE-add ask under rule 14).** BW's ask (trim NVDA ~2.4pp, trim VTI ~3.3pp, add XLE ~1.5-2.5pp) is a genuine, consistently-argued risk read (look-through tech concentration ~27% of equity, unchanged for four straight reports) but it is not a fired mechanical trigger — both concentration triggers (18-20% single-name, 25% combined) stayed clean throughout — and MS's own valuation view does not independently endorse it: NVDA's DCF is static-overvalued with no add/trim case either way, and XLE's DCF *flipped* to mildly overvalued (-1.8%) this run, undercutting the "add" half of BW's own ask (BW itself acknowledged this, handing the XLE sizing question to BR/MS rather than repeating an unqualified "add" a third time). BR — the desk that owns allocation-policy targets — has been silent since 9/16 ~16:14 ET, now 23+ hours and 5+ hours past its own self-committed cross-desk-vet deadline (committed to fire the moment a second consecutive F landed, which happened at 10:42 ET today). Per rule 14, this gets an explicit decision now rather than a fourth re-flag: **declined for today, not adopted.** Rationale: this book has never actually executed a rebalance on one desk's repeated word alone. The closest-sounding precedent (the 8/6-8/7 VTI trim) is easy to misremember as "BR's silence became the trigger," but the actual record shows BR broke its silence with a fresh, independently-converging report *before* any silence-based fallback fired — that fallback was written down but never actually used. Sustained repetition from one desk is not the same as cross-desk convergence, and rule 6 exists precisely because a sizing decision needs more than one voice. **Standing resolution going forward:** a discretionary (non-triggered) rebalance ask needs BR's actual sign-off to execute, not a silence-implied license to act; if BR's silence stretches into a genuinely extended gap (multiple days, not hours), that is a process problem worth naming to Jash directly (next weekly summary) rather than grounds to self-authorize a trade in the meantime. No trade resulted from this resolution — GEHC also ticked to $65.02 this run, just above the top of its $62-65 continuation band on no fresh catalyst (a WebSearch "guidance cut" headline traced to the already-priced-in April 2026 Q1 print via stale law-firm securities-investigation spam, not a new development, per rule 4) — logged as a tape-only drift per BW's own flag, not actioned.

**Rule 19 validated the very next day, 2026-09-17 ~16:11 ET.** BR finally broke its silence (~5.5 hours after its own self-committed trigger condition) and resolved BW's repeated NVDA/VTI-trim, XLE-add ask — not via BW's literal sizing, but via a policy-target revision (NVDA 11%→10%, VTI 30%→28%, XLE 10%→12%, Cash 10%→11% of pool), the same "encode the structural finding into policy, no forced same-day trade" mechanism first used on the 8/27 NVDA-target question. This is the second time this exact resolution pattern has worked: a repeated, well-argued risk-desk finding gets converted into standing policy by the allocation owner, without ever requiring a pressured, same-day sizing trade. The same report created a new, falsifiable, time-boxed XLE top-up trigger ($2-3 cash-funded, needs XLE non-extending + MS's composite gap flat-to-better than -1.8%, time-boxed to the ~10/1 underwrite) — which then failed cleanly on both legs the very next trading day (9/18: MS's gap widened to -2.1%, and BW/GS independently confirmed the funding leg can't clear BR's own new 11% reserve floor without a design fix). A trigger failing its first live test isn't itself a flaw — it's rule 7 working exactly as designed (no fire without both conditions actually met) — but the funding-math hole is a real, twice-flagged design gap that needs fixing (a smaller top-up size, or an explicit floor exception) before the trigger's next live test, not discovered under pressure in the moment.

**Rule 19's third validated case, 2026-10-19 ~16:1x ET — and a process lesson about a resolved ask getting re-litigated as if it were still open.** BW's AI/semis look-through-concentration ask (new core capital should tilt away from VTI, which embeds most of the look-through AI/mega-tech exposure) had repeated for 3+ cycles by mid-October without an explicit adopt/decline. BR resolved it the same way as the two prior cases: not via a forced trade, but a policy-target revision — core VTI/VXUS pool targets moved 28%/25% → 26%/27%, with OMCL and GEHC explicitly declined as tilt vehicles (each keeps its own existing mechanism; the fix only touches where *future* core dollars land). No trade was forced; both VTI and VXUS sat inside their drift bands under either the old or new targets, and rule 6a's rate-shock pause continued to block any new core purchase regardless of which ETF it would nominally favor. **The process lesson, caught at this 10/23 distillation:** BW's own 10/23 ~14:42 ET report continued to frame this exact ask as "unresolved… across several cycles" and "the one BR has now been asked to adopt or decline," four days after BR had already adopted it — even though BR's own 10/22 ~16:12 ET report explicitly lists the revised 26%/27% targets as adopted policy. A desk can keep citing a finding as open after the allocation owner has already converted it into policy, simply by not cross-checking the other desk's most recent report before restating its own standing ask. The fix isn't a new rule — it's a reminder that rule 19's "check whether this was already resolved" step applies to reading the other analysts' reports just as much as to the trader's own decision-making, and that a repeated-sounding ask deserves a quick verification pass (has the allocation owner already acted on this?) before being logged as a live gap in a weekly distillation. No sizing consequence either way; BW's newer, distinct ask this week (revisit trimming NVDA's own equity weight toward ~10% once the FOMC/earnings window clears) is a different, not-yet-repeated suggestion and stays an open item (see below), not folded into this resolution.

20. **A sustained single-position drift does not automatically get encoded into policy the way rule 19's "revise the target" precedent might suggest — the allocation owner can instead decide the drift itself is undesirable and respond with a redeployment instruction (formalized 2026-10-02, from BR's 10/1 ~16:13 ET NVDA-target resolution).** Rule 19's prior two live tests (the 8/27 NVDA 15%→11% question, the 9/17 NVDA/VTI/XLE target revision) both resolved a repeated flag by raising/lowering a target to match where the position had organically drifted — "encode the finding into policy, no forced trade." This time, with NVDA running +1.4-1.6pp over its 10% target for 8+ consecutive days at the exact same time MS's DCF gap on the name hit its widest-ever reading (-16.7%), BR explicitly declined to raise the target to match the overshoot, reasoning that doing so "at the exact moment the valuation and sentiment case against the name both deteriorate further would be pro-cyclical, reward-chasing allocation, not policy discipline." The target stayed at 10%, paired with a standing instruction to route no new deployable cash to NVDA until the overshoot closes on its own (via price reverting or other sleeves growing). The lesson: rule 19's "convert a repeated finding into policy" mechanism is not a rubber stamp for whatever the market has already done — the allocation owner still has to ask whether ratifying the drift is sound allocation or just chasing it, and the two prior tests happening to answer "ratify" doesn't make that the default answer. A third, symmetric case (declining to chase a drift downward, not just upward) hasn't been tested yet.

**Retired/superseded:** OMCL's original three-trigger entry framework (closed 7/17); XLE's entry-zone trigger (closed 7/28, now a live half-size holding under standard ongoing-position discipline); the VTI overweight "BR vs. BW" disagreement (closed 8/7 — BR ended its silence with a $2.00 trim recommendation that BW independently converged on; see rule 12/13); BW's repeated NVDA pre-earnings de-risking-trim ask (closed 8/14 — declined, see rule 15 and the NVDA earnings contingency plan section above); the Gmail "drafts only, no send tool" limitation (resolved 8/14 — mcp__Gmail__send_message now works; the 8/14 weekly summary was actually sent, not drafted); the GEHC entry trigger (closed 9/3 — fired on a genuine pullback inside its ≤$70 ceiling, see GEHC entry trigger section above, now a live quarter-size holding under standard ongoing-position discipline); IONQ's rule-6 gate (closed 9/4 — MS's first-ever DCF delivered a hard pass, ~83% downside; GS formally retired it from its active-idea list the same day, see rule 5).

**Operational note:** local git HEAD detaching from `main` between runs, or local `main` sitting stale/diverged from `origin/main`, remains a recurring container quirk (not a one-off) — observed repeatedly through late July and August, and again multiple times this week (9/2, 9/3, 9/4). It has never once caused real data loss when caught before editing: the standard fix (`git checkout main && git pull origin main`, or `git fetch origin main && git reset --hard origin/main` when that fails or diverges, or `git branch -f main HEAD && git checkout main` for a clean detached-HEAD-matching-origin case) always resolves it. Always run this as the very first step of every run, and don't trust a clean `git status` alone — confirm the latest commit hash/date matches what's expected before editing. **New this week (2026-09-04): a separate, previously-undocumented environment constraint** — this session's network egress proxy blocks direct fetches to primary data sources (fred.stlouisfed.org, home.treasury.gov, federalreserve.gov, cnbc.com all returned blocked/failed on a 9/4 12:36 ET attempt). WebSearch remains reachable and is the only rate/data-verification channel available here; when a WebSearch-sourced figure is itself ambiguous (as the 9/3 10yr close was, four figures across four runs), there is currently no way to break the tie with a primary source from inside this environment. Worth a standing instruction to Jash: if a future rate-shock or similarly precision-sensitive trigger needs unambiguous resolution, either grant this environment egress to a primary data source or accept that WebSearch-snippet convergence (multiple independent sources agreeing) is the practical ceiling on precision here. **New this week (2026-09-07): a genuine timestamp-mislabeling bug, not just a git-state quirk.** Cross-checking today's self-reported "~HH:MM ET" labels against the actual git commit timestamps (`git show --format=%ai -s <hash>`) found that GS's 13:45 report, MS's 14:15 report, and this desk's own prior 13:36 run note had all self-labeled raw UTC clock time as "ET" — a ~4-hour offset (real ET at each of those commits was ~9:44, ~10:15, and ~9:38 respectively). JPM's same-morning report was the exception, correctly landing close to real ET. Real ET is confirmed the direct way: bash `TZ=America/New_York date`. This run's own note below uses that verified real time rather than perpetuating the offset. Practical impact so far is limited — every hard time-box or trigger in this book's history has been date-level, not hour-level — but any future same-day, hour-sensitive sequencing call (e.g., "which report is freshest this run") should verify real ET directly rather than trust a prior self-labeled timestamp at face value. **New this week (2026-09-14): the git-state quirk produced a more severe variant than any prior instance.** Instead of a simple detached-HEAD or stale-but-fast-forwardable `main`, this run found local `main` ~50 commits diverged from `origin/main` with **no common merge base at all** (`git merge-base main origin/main` returned nothing) — the local branch's own history didn't connect to origin's at any point `git` could find. The existing fix still worked: `git checkout -B main origin/main` force-aligns local `main` to origin (the source of truth every session actually reads/writes) without needing a real merge, and cost nothing since the diverged local commits were all old, already-superseded duplicates of history origin/main carries further along anyway. Lesson: when `git merge-base` comes back empty, don't try to reconcile or merge the two histories — just force-align to `origin/main` directly, exactly as if the branch didn't exist locally yet. **New this week (2026-09-18): a file-size limit was actually hit, not just a growth trend flagged in the abstract.** trading-experiment/state.md reached ~715KB (only 484 lines, but individual lines running 1-6KB of dense prose) and exceeded this session's Read tool's 256KB single-read limit outright — this run had to read the file in offset/limit chunks and delegate part of the historical digest to a subagent to stay within context. Root cause: the weekly-pruning practice (step 7 of the standing routine) only ever authorized trimming the "Run notes / news sweeps" section; the separate "Balance history" section has been carrying every entry since at least 2026-09-01 (likely further back) without ever being pruned, and it is the larger of the two sections by a wide margin. This run did not unilaterally start pruning Balance history, since the standing instructions don't authorize it and doing so would be a memory-architecture change, not a trading decision — flagged instead to Jash in this week's summary email for an explicit decision (extend the same trim-to-a-week practice to Balance history too, with full detail preserved in git history exactly as Run notes already does, or some other fix). Future runs: if state.md ever fails to Read in full again, use offset/limit chunks (as this run did) rather than treating it as a blocking failure, and re-flag the underlying file-size question if Jash's decision is still pending. **New, higher-severity finding (2026-09-29 ~15:41 ET): a "git hygiene: resolved, confirmed current" self-report in a run note is not proof the fix actually happened — verify the remote tip directly.** This run found local git history diverged from `origin/main` by a full 6 days (50 commits, 9/24-9/29, no common ancestor), even though six of today's own prior run notes each separately claimed to have resolved a divergence via `git checkout -B main origin/main` / `git reset --hard origin/main` / `git merge --ff-only origin/main` and confirmed "current." None of those fixes actually reached `origin/main` (still sitting at the 9/23 13:37 ET commit) — the likely mechanism is `git push origin main` silently pushing a stale local `main` branch ref (reporting "up-to-date"/exit 0) while `HEAD` stayed detached on the real, unpushed work, so a clean `git status` plus a reported success message gave false confidence. **The fix used on 9/14 (force-align local `main` to `origin/main`, discarding local commits, on the reasoning they're old superseded duplicates) is only safe when that's actually true — verify it first.** This time the diverged commits were the *only* copy of 6 days of real run history, so force-aligning would have destroyed data instead of routinely fixing a quirk. Before applying that fix again: run `git diff --stat <origin/main> <local HEAD>` and confirm the changes really are superseded/duplicate content, not unique unpushed work. **Standing practice going forward: at the start of every run, verify `origin/main`'s actual latest commit date/hash (not just `git status`) before doing anything else**, and if a real, non-superseded divergence is found, do not force-discard it — push it to a preservation branch (`trader-memory`, per the routine's own rejected-push fallback) immediately, before any other step, since this container is ephemeral and unpushed local commits can be lost permanently between runs. This incident was resolved by pushing the orphaned history to `origin/trader-memory` and escalating to Jash by email rather than by either discarding it or forcing a git-history rewrite (this session's safety controls declined to auto-approve the merge-commit repair, correctly treating it as a human decision) — full detail in the 9/29 ~15:41 ET run note. **RESOLVED 2026-10-30 (~15:37 ET distillation): the Balance-history pruning question, raised in 9 consecutive weekly summaries with no reply, was executed per the standing default stated in the 2026-10-26 run notes.** Both Balance history and Run notes were trimmed to a 7-day rolling window (entries dated before 2026-10-23 removed from the live file); file size dropped from ~1.5MB/790 lines to ~410KB/~350 lines. All removed detail remains fully intact in git history (`git log -p -- trading-experiment/state.md`) — nothing was lost, only moved out of the hot file. One structural wrinkle worth flagging for whoever prunes next: both sections had silently drifted out of strict chronological order over time (a reverse-chronological block mixed with one or more differently-ordered append blocks, likely residue from past merge-conflict resolutions) — a naive line-by-line regex trim breaks on this, because an older multi-line/indented entry's continuation lines don't themselves match a "drop this" pattern and get orphaned. The safe method (used this run): parse the file maintaining a per-line "current entry date" state (an entry starts at a line matching `^- 2026-MM-DD`; every line until the next such match, including blanks, belongs to that same entry), and drop whole entries by date rather than individual lines. Verified post-prune: zero orphaned fragments, section headers intact, 79 entries retained (dated 2026-10-23 through 2026-10-30).

A trade *through* $35.24 remains the explicit line for a mandatory re-read per the OMCL earnings contingency plan — proximity alone isn't the trigger. Moot for this book either way — SNDK is unheld and MS's base case is overvalued under both readings — but flagged for MS to re-verify against a live-sourced price next cycle. (4) **The OMCL DCA gate** (rule 18) continues to oscillate with the pool's own swings (ranged roughly $1.5-2.5 away from the $2.50 threshold this week, ending the week wider as today's selloff ate into accumulated profit) without firing, now 8+ weeks running as the closest live mechanical trigger. (5) **AVGO remains GS's one not-yet-vetted top pick**; ALAB's price staleness is a closed loop (MS's hard pass is robust to it, confirmed 10/13/10/23). (6) **BW's NVDA-trim-to-~10%-equity-weight idea (first raised 10/19) hasn't recurred since** — lapsed for now, not a rule-14 case unless it resurfaces. (7) **BR's cadence, which genuinely improved 10/19-10/22, reverted this week** — only one report since Wednesday (10/29 ~16:13 ET), ~23 hours stale as of this distillation; last week's "does the improvement hold past the FOMC week" question is answered: not yet, worth naming directly in this week's summary rather than re-flagging silently again.

- **GS Stock Screener** — Steady, process-oriented week: resolved ALAB's price-conflict flag via a live Robinhood cross-check (10/20), then escalated the same name's price staleness from a watch item to an explicit process flag (10/23) when fresh pulls kept coming up empty for 3+ weeks — the right instinct both times (fix it when you can, name it loudly when you can't).
