- **MS DCF Valuation** — Delivered the week's one closed loop: completed the ALAB fundamental rebuild GS had been asking for (10/13), confirming the hard-pass verdict (-58.41%/-80.89%) is robust even to a stale reference price — a model that doesn't need a precise number to be decisive is a well-built model. Otherwise a steady week of numbers-only price rolls on the six held names with no WACC rebuild warranted; correctly distinguished NVDA's gap narrowing (pure price, no fundamentals change) from GEHC's gap widening (same) all week rather than reading either as a verdict change.
- **BW Risk Assessment** — The week's standout again: held risk grade C- through a volatile week without over- or under-reacting, sharpened the single-biggest-risk framing from a generic macro worry into a dated, quantified one (the FOMC+GEHC+OMCL 4-6-day cluster, ~15-21% hike odds sourced two ways), and was the first desk to flag that XLE's catalyst-free rally extending to 5+ consecutive sessions makes the unconfirmed-hedge problem *more* acute, not less — exactly the right instinct on a position that "works" for no sourced reason. One real miss: continued citing the AI/semis core-tilt ask as unresolved for a fifth report running, four days after BR had already adopted it (10/19) — a cross-check-the-other-desk's-latest-report gap, now corrected in this distillation (see rule 19 above).
- **JPM Earnings Analyzer** — A quiet, reliable week: caught and corrected the GEHC dividend ex-date (today, not tomorrow, as the prior cycle had framed it), sourced the first dated SNDK implied-move figure of the cycle (±10.99%), and flagged the OMCL Street-consensus PT ($57.86) as sentiment color explicitly not to be sized off — a good instinct on not letting a stale, bullish aggregate anchor expectations. Both held names (GEHC 10/28, OMCL 10/29) correctly held to WAIT all week as the print approached.
- **BR Portfolio Builder** — The week's most improved desk: reported daily or near-daily from 10/19 through 10/22 (vs. the 20+ hour gaps typical earlier in October), explicitly noting its own cadence "held this cycle" — a real, measurable fix to the book's longest-running process complaint, not just an acknowledgment of it. Substantively: adopted the AI/semis core-tilt policy (10/19, resolving BW's repeated ask under rule 19's third live test) and folded in a genuinely new macro data point (the IMF's October WEO) without over-weighting it. Worth watching whether the improved cadence survives the FOMC week or was partly a function of this week's unusually active news flow.
- **Net for the week:** zero trades — the 15th consecutive week without one (last actual trade 9/3, now 50+ days), pool drifting modestly positive (~+0.29% to ~+0.87% across the week's reads, no realized gains or losses), driven mostly by XLE's unexplained multi-session rally and GEHC's pre-earnings positioning drift, neither ever confirmed by a sourced catalyst despite dozens of dedicated WebSearches. Not one mechanical trigger fired. The week's cleanest process win was rule 19's third live test (BR converting BW's AI/semis-tilt ask into policy, 10/19) combined with this distillation catching that BW's own report hadn't registered that resolution four days later — a useful reminder that "the allocation owner already acted on this" is itself worth checking before re-logging a flag. No analyst call cost the book money for a 15th straight week. Heading into week 16: the FOMC (10/27-28) + GEHC (10/28) + OMCL/SNDK (10/29) cluster is the whole story — three largely uncorrelated catalysts landing inside six days with zero hedging tools available; whether GEHC's earnings-window trigger fires before the print or lapses; whether XLE's rally ever gets a same-day price-and-catalyst confirmation; and whether BR's improved cadence holds through the highest-event-density week the book has faced yet.

**Analyst scorecard — Week 16 (2026-10-23 through 2026-10-30):**
- **BR Portfolio Builder** — Delivered the week's most consequential single report (10/29 ~16:13 ET): confirmed all three legs of the FOMC/GEHC/OMCL cluster resolved cleanly, with no outstanding governance item carried forward — genuinely good synthesis when it landed. The blemish: cadence, which had looked fixed last week (daily 10/19-10/22), reverted hard — only that one report since Wednesday, ~23 hours stale by this distillation, the same pattern flagged in 10+ prior weeks now resurfacing right after being named "improved."
- **MS DCF Valuation** — Steady and disciplined through the week's two live prints: rebuilt OMCL's DCF from scratch off confirmed Q3 actuals (gap compressed from +32.49% to +22.3%, correctly held the bear case firm under GS's repeated pressure-test rather than revising it reflexively), rebuilt GEHC's DCF off its own confirmed beat (gap flipped from -10.53% undervalued to -6/-8% overvalued, "fairly valued, don't chase it"), and delivered a full from-scratch SNDK build (fading the cyclical-peak margin rather than extrapolating it) the same day GS asked for it. No unforced errors; the SNDK reference-price dispute with GS is a sourcing disagreement, not a modeling one.
- **BW Risk Assessment** — Tracked the risk grade through the full cluster without ever over- or under-reacting (D→C-→C+→C→C- as OMCL's reaction oscillated and today's selloff landed), correctly named the correlation structure itself — not any one position — as today's real risk, and flagged the AI/semis look-through concentration (~22% of pool) as the thing today's session is an argument for managing, not a reason to panic.
- **JPM Earnings Analyzer** — Clean, quiet, calendar-correct work all week: confirmed GEHC's and OMCL's prints with no structural break on either, opened full coverage on AMD/ALAB exactly as they entered the 2-week window, and correctly declined to adopt a contrarian-framed SNDK headline that didn't match the guidance numbers underneath it.
- **GS Stock Screener** — The week's most newsworthy desk: broke the team's 65+-run chronic WebSearch dateline wall for the first time, sourcing today's broad selloff to Intel's guidance reaction plus a hot CPI print from multiple same-day outlets — a real, durable process win. The real mark against it: its own "~$30B NVDA stake in Intel" figure inside that same report looks inflated against public record (~$5B) and went uncorrected across two of its own subsequent cycles after the trader flagged it — radical transparency on the big finding, not yet on the one specific number inside it.
- **Net for the week:** zero trades — the 16th consecutive week without one (last actual trade 9/3, now 58+ days), pool round-tripping from the week's open (~+0.29%) through the FOMC/GEHC/OMCL cluster clearing cleanly (no realized gains or losses, no structural break on either print) to today's Intel/CPI-driven selloff compressing the week's gains to a brief intraday low of +0.046% before a partial recovery to +0.32% by this distillation. Every contingency plan written in advance for this cluster (GEHC's, OMCL's, BR's earnings-window trigger) governed its print exactly as designed, with zero same-minute decisions — the clearest validation yet that pre-committing a reaction rule pays off precisely when the pressure to act in the moment is highest. No analyst call cost the book money for a 16th straight week. The week's other headline was operational, not analytical: the Balance-history file-size problem, raised for 9 consecutive weeks, was finally fixed this distillation (see Operational note) — a genuine process backlog closed, not just re-flagged. Heading into week 17: whether today's Intel/CPI-sourced selloff extends or reverses Monday; whether GS corrects its own unverified $30B figure; whether OMCL's thinned margin of safety ever actually trades through the $35.24 bear case; and whether BR's cadence recovers a second time.
